> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The three hooks form a closed experiential loop:**

1. **Post-submission** (`bash` event on `popcorn.*submit`) — captures results at the moment they happen. This is the "write" path — new data flows into kernel skills immediately after each experiment.

2. **Stop-time learn check** (`stop` event) — catches anything that fell through the cracks. This is the "sweep" — a final pass before context is lost. Evaluates whether `/learn` should extract a reusable pattern.

3. **Skill cross-ref** (`file` event on kernel skill edits) — maintains consistency across the 6-skill ecosystem. This is the "propagation" path — when one skill gets new data, the hub and companions stay in sync.

Without these hooks, the failure mode was: results discovered in session N get lost, and session N+3 retries the same dead end. The MoE `doweight_stage1=True` was retried 3+ times across sessions before being permanently documented. These hooks prevent that.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

All three hooks are active immediately — no restart needed.

| Hook | Triggers On | Action |
|------|------------|--------|
| `post-submission-learning` | Any `popcorn submit/benchmark/leaderboard` command | Warn: record result in kernel skill |
| `stop-learn-check` | Session stop | Warn: evaluate for `/learn` extraction |
| `skill-crossref-check` | Editing any kernel skill SKILL.md | Warn: update hub + companions |

The recursive experiential loop is now: **experiment → record (hook 1) → propagate (hook 3) → sweep (hook 2) → next session starts with complete knowledge**.

Agents are still running (MoE and MLA in progress). Anything else to set up while we wait?

MoE Gate 2 result is in — and it's significant.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**MoE active-expert masking: CRASHED with GPU memory fault — but with NEW diagnostic data.**

Key findings from this submission:
1. **The expert mask probe worked** — `[gate2] num_experts=257 active=55 bs=8 topk=9` — only 55 of 257 experts are active. This confirms the sparse dispatch hypothesis.
2. **But then GPU memory fault** on `ck_moe_stage1` — same address pattern as Phase 18's crash. The fault happens AFTER JIT builds complete, DURING the actual CK kernel execution.
3. **JIT builds still ran** (sorting 25.7s + CK 114.8s = 140.5s) — this means AITER_JIT_DIR either wasn't set or didn't persist. Gate 1 (JIT cache) appears to have been skipped or failed.
4. **The `moe_sorting_fwd` signature reveals `local_expert_mask` parameter!** — `local_expert_mask: Optional[torch.Tensor] = None`. This is the NATIVE expert masking path — no need for custom clamping. But the crash happened before this could be tested.
5. **Timeout at 540s** — JIT (140s) + crash recovery + timeout = 540s total.

The most actionable discovery: `moe_sorting_fwd` natively supports `local_expert_mask`. Previous attempts tried to mask AFTER sorting. The correct approach is to pass the mask TO the sorting function.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Let me record this finding in the MoE skill per our new hookify rule.

### Learning Evaluation (Phase 1.1)

## K-Search Implementation & Paper Alignment Report

I've completed a thorough search of the codebase for K-Search references and implementations. Here's what exists and where the gaps are:

### **What Exists**

**1. Skill Documentation** (`~/.claude/skills/k-search-llm-kernel-optimization/SKILL.md`)
- ✅ Complete K-Search framework documentation
- ✅ Reference to arxiv 2602.19128 (UC Berkeley, 2026)
- ✅ Paper's core concepts documented: tree search, action selection, stagnation detection (K=7), world model co-evolution
- ✅ MoE-specific optimization strategies (warp-level routing, token reordering, tensor core utilization, expert skipping)
- ✅ Application mapping to AMD MI355X hardware
- ✅ Manual methodology for applying K-Search without full framework

**2. Autonomous Implementation** (`~/dev/cohezion/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/autoresearch/`)
   
   **Core Files (1,063 total LOC):**
   - **`ksearch_tree.py`** (178 LOC): K-Search tree with KNode, priority-based selection
     - ✅ `KNode`: strategy, parameters, priority (0.0-1.0), stagnation_count, status
     - ✅ `select_best()`: Leaf-first, priority-ordered node selection (implements Phase 1 Action Selection)
     - ✅ `update_result()`: Tracks stagnation, auto-prunes at K=7 threshold (implements Phase 2 results)
     - ✅ `decay_priorities()`: Periodic exploration encouragement
     - ✅ `mark_failed()`: Failure pruning
     
   - **`driver.py`** (357 LOC): Main autonomous loop
     - ✅ Cycle-based execution: SELECT → generate → test → benchmark → UPDATE → log
     - ✅ Kernel rotation with weighted priorities (MoE 50%, GEMM 30%, MLA 20%)
     - ✅ Rate limiting (1 leaderboard/hour/problem)
     - ✅ Parameter perturbation on subsequent attempts (±KSPLIT for MoE, ±log2_ks for GEMM, etc.)
     - ✅ Convergence detection (stops if <1% improvement in 10 cycles)
     - ✅ Emergency backup/restore on failures
   
   - **`analyzer.py`** (141 LOC): Result analysis → tree updates
     - ✅ Computes geomean, bottleneck identification, improvement %
     - ✅ Calls `tree.update_result()` for tree co-evolution
     - ✅ Per-shape ranking and suggestions
   
   - **`generator.py`** (98 LOC): Template instantiation
     - ✅ String.Template substitution for per-shape lookup tables
     - ✅ MoE: KSPLIT_TABLE (shape-specific values)
     - ✅ GEMM: KERNEL_TABLE (shape-specific kernel + split-K)
     - ✅ MLA: SPLITS_TABLE (shape-specific num_kv_splits)
   
   - **`evaluator.py`** (232 LOC): popcorn-cli wrapper
     - ✅ Test (correctness), benchmark (per-shape timing), leaderboard (official score)
   
   - **Pre-seeded K-Search Trees:**
     - `tree/moe_tree.json`: 11 nodes (E=257/33, bs=16/128/512, KSPLIT strategies)
     - `tree/gemm_tree.json`: 11 nodes (M=4/256, split-K strategies)
     - `tree/mla_tree.json`: 14 nodes (bs/kv_seqlen, num_splits, KV format)
   
   - **Results Logging:**
     - `results/moe_runs.jsonl`, `gemm_runs.jsonl`, `mla_runs.jsonl` (append-only crash-safe logs)

### **What's Implemented from the Paper**

| Paper Component | Implementation | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **Phase 1: Action Selection** | `ksearch_tree.select_best()` — max(priority) from active nodes | ✅ Full |
| **Phase 2: Program Instantiation (K=7)** | `driver.py` loop with stagnation tracking | ✅ Full (K hardcoded to 7) |
| **Phase 3: World Model Co-Evolution** | `analyzer.update_result()` + `tree.decay_priorities()` | ⚠️ Partial |
| **V-Score (Priority 0.0-1.0)** | `KNode.priority` field | ✅ Present |
| **Stagnation Detection (K=7)** | `KNode.stagnation_count` + auto-prune at threshold | ✅ Full |
| **Tree persistence** | JSON save/load with atomic writes | ✅ Full |
| **Per-shape configuration** | Lookup tables in templates (KSPLIT_TABLE, etc.) | ✅ Full |

### **What's Missing vs. Paper Methodology**

**Critical Gaps:**

1. **No Explicit LLM Components (pi_plan, pi_code)**
   - Paper describes two LLM policies: 
     - `pi_plan` (world model): Reasons about WHAT strategies to try, maintains search tree
     - `pi_code` (implementation): Generates concrete kernel code from strategy
   - **Implementation**: Tree and code generation are deterministic/templated, not LLM-guided
   - **Gap**: No LLM to propose new strategies mid-run; strategies are pre-seeded and fixed

2. **World Model Updates (Phase 3) Incomplete**
   - Paper: INSERT new child nodes extending successful branches with refined optimization intents
   - Paper: UPDATE priority scores based on accumulated evidence
   - Paper: PRUNE infeasible branches to concentrate budget
   - **Implementation**: Only updates priorities and marks exhausted/pruned; does NOT dynamically INSERT new strategies or PRUNE branches based on learned patterns
   - **Code**: `analyzer.update_result()` calls `tree.update_result()` which only updates best_result_us and stagnation_count, not INSERT/PRUNE logic

3. **In-Context Learning Window**
   - Paper: World model sees full trajectory of attempts and results
   - **Implementation**: Tree only stores per-node statistics (best_result_us, stagnation_count), not trajectory of attempts
   - **Missing**: Historical replay buffer or full attempt trajectory for retrospection

4. **Dynamic Strategy Generation**
   - Paper: Figure 2 shows pi_plan proposing new child strategies after observing results
   - **Implementation**: Strategies are hardcoded in JSON trees; no mechanism to INSERT new strategies mid-run
   - **Current**: Fixed strategy tree with ~11 nodes per kernel; only parameter perturbation happens

5. **Tree Insert/Update/Prune Lifecycle**
   - Paper: Explicit operations:
     - **INSERT**: Add child nodes with new optimization intents
     - **UPDATE**: Re-evaluate V scores dynamically
     - **PRUNE**: Remove infeasible branches
   - **Implementation**: Only PRUNE on exhaustion (status → pruned); no dynamic INSERT or strategic PRUNE based on analysis
   - **Code Location**: `ksearch_tree.py` has `mark_failed()` but no `insert_child_strategies()` based on results

6. **V-Score Decay Mechanism**
   - Paper: Priority scores updated dynamically based on improvement trajectory
   - **Implementation**: 
     - Linear boost on improvement: `priority = min(1.0, priority + 0.1 * improvement_pct)`
     - Fixed decay: `priority *= 0.95` every 5 cycles (not adaptive to learning)
   - **Gap**: No Bayesian updating, no confidence bounds, no exploration/exploitation scheduling

7. **Bottleneck-Driven Tree Expansion**
   - Paper suggests tree should adapt based on shape-specific bottlenecks
   - **Implementation**: Analysis identifies bottleneck shape but doesn't trigger INSERT of shape-specific child nodes
   - **Example**: If shape E=257,bs=16 is bottleneck, paper would INSERT new children targeting that shape; code only logs suggestion

8. **Failure Mode Classification**
   - Paper: Distinguishes implementation defects from strategy failure
   - Paper quotes: "If `torch.compile(fused_moe)` crashes, that's an implementation failure — the strategy 'reduce Python dispatch overhead' is still valid"
   - **Implementation**: `mark_failed()` marks entire node as pruned without distinguishing correctness failure vs. implementation defect
   - **Gap**: No retry-with-different-implementation logic for failed strategies

### **Current Search Tree State (from `moe_tree.json`)**

```
ROOT: Minimize fused_moe geomean (priority=0.95, attempts=0)
├─ moe_n1: Per-shape KSPLIT lookup table (V=0.9025, attempts=0)
│  ├─ moe_n1a: E=257,d=256,bs=16: sweep KSPLIT 1-6 (V=0.855)
│  ├─ moe_n1b: E=257,d=256,bs=128: sweep KSPLIT 0-6
│  ├─ moe_n1c: E=257,d=256,bs=512: sweep KSPLIT 0-1
│  ├─ moe_n1d: E=33,d=512,bs=16: sweep KSPLIT 1-4
│  └─ moe_n1e: E=33,d=512,bs=128: sweep KSPLIT 1-6
├─ moe_n2: [CLOSED, V=0.0] Parameter tuning — exhausted Phase 1-17
├─ moe_n3: [CLOSED, V=0.0] doweight_stage1 — GPU fault + 82% mismatch
└─ moe_n4: [CLOSED, V=0.0] Custom Triton MoE — 68% slower than CK ASM
```

**Status:** All nodes are hardcoded pre-seeds; none dynamically inserted during run.

### **What Would Be Needed for Full K-Search Paper Implementation**

1. **LLM Integration** (High effort)
   - Call Claude/GPT to suggest new strategies based on tree state
   - Use pi_code LLM to generate kernel code variants from strategy descriptions
   - Maintain in-context trajectory for world model learning

2. **Dynamic Tree Operations** (Medium effort)
   ```python
   # In analyzer.py:
   def analyze_and_evolve(result, tree, node):
       # Current: just update priority
       tree.update_result(node.id, geomean)
       
       # Paper: INSERT new children based on bottleneck
       if bottleneck_shape and improvement > 5%:
           tree.insert_child(
               node.id,
               strategy=f"Focus on {bottleneck_shape}",
               parameters={...refined...},
               priority=0.8  # Optimistic for new branch
           )
       
       # Paper: PRUNE low-confidence branches
       if node.stagnation_count >= K:
           tree.mark_failed(node.id, "stagnation")
           # Also prune siblings if parent exhausted
   ```

3. **Trajectory Replay Buffer** (Low-medium effort)
   - Store full attempt history (not just best_result)
   - Use for retrospection: "Which parameter ranges consistently improved?"
   - Enable llm.in_context_learning(trajectory) for world model updates

4. **Adaptive V-Score Scheduling** (Medium effort)
   - Replace fixed 0.1 boost with Bayesian confidence bounds
   - Implement UCB/Thompson sampling for exploration/exploitation trade-off
   - Decay based on information gain, not wall-clock time

5. **Shape-Specific Tree Refinement** (Low effort)
   - When bottleneck identified, INSERT targeted child strategies
   - Example: If shape X is 30% of geomean, create dedicated subtree for it

## R-Zero and RL Kernel Optimization: Codebase Analysis

Based on a thorough exploration of the cohezion codebase, here's what exists and what's missing regarding R-Zero, reinforcement learning for GPU kernel optimization, and the methodology from chengsong-huang.github.io/R-Zero:

---

### **What EXISTS in the Codebase**

#### 1. **R-Zero Framework (Conceptual)**
- **File:** `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/docs/archive/RESEARCH_PAPER_DRAFT.md`
- **Status:** Implemented as a co-evolutionary control framework
- **Components:**
  - **Challenger Agent**: Queries memory for historical variance; increments difficulty index (𝒟) when solver plateaus
  - **Solver Agent**: Retrieves tools and past successful strategies; generates solutions
  - **Pragmatist Agent**: Enforces constitutional boundaries and stylistic rules
- **Key Insight:** The system demonstrates a linear relationship between Difficulty Index (𝒟) and Agentic Coherence, suggesting "anti-fragility" can be engineered into training loops
- **Verified:** Was scored 100/100 in the skill evaluation report as `R_ZERO_CHALLENGER_PRIME.md`

#### 2. **Reward Model Infrastructure**
- **File:** `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/universe/llm_training_bridge.py`
- **Status:** Production-grade reward system integrated with 12D universe simulations
- **Components:**
  - `TrajectoryToReward`: Converts 12D universe trajectories into scalar reward signals
  - `PreferencePairGenerator`: Generates DPO (Direct Preference Optimization) training pairs
  - `JudgmentEvaluator`: Assesses agent decision quality against HIHO-optimal actions
  - `ExperienceDataset`: Packages trajectories into LLM training-ready format (JSONL)
- **Reward Components:**
  - HIHO proximity (0.4 weight): Distance to 0.5 coherence ideal
  - SPIN alignment bonus (0.2): Rotation/precession phase alignment
  - Tempic stability (0.1): Rate-of-change of agent state
  - Precipitation bonus (0.2): Did the agent produce useful output?
  - Consistency (0.1): Coherence variance across trajectory
- **Output Formats:** JSONL files for DPO, reward model training, judgment fine-tuning

#### 3. **K-Search Tree Optimization (Active Kernel Optimization)**
- **Files:** `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/autoresearch/`
- **Status:** Autonomous overnight kernel optimization loop for AMD E2E Model Speedrun
- **Architecture:**
  - **K-Search Tree** (`ksearch_tree.py`): Tree-structured optimization with SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/PRUNE operations
  - **Template Generator** (`generator.py`): Parameterized kernel templates with variable substitution
  - **Evaluator** (`evaluator.py`): Wraps popcorn-cli for test/benchmark/leaderboard submission
  - **Analyzer** (`analyzer.py`): Analyzes results, updates tree priorities, suggests next experiments
  - **Rate Limiter** (`rate_limiter.py`): 1 leaderboard submission/hour/problem
- **Key Innovation:** Per-shape lookup tables instead of one-size-fits-all parameters
  - MoE: KSPLIT table (0-6 per shape)
  - GEMM: Kernel selection + split-K (log2_ks 0-4)
  - MLA: Adaptive num_kv_splits per (batch_size, kv_seq_len)
- **Search Strategy:**
  - Priority-weighted kernel rotation (MoE 50%, GEMM 30%, MLA 20% based on gap-to-leader)
  - Parameter perturbation on subsequent attempts
  - Stagnation-based pruning (K=7 threshold)
  - Auto-decay of priorities to encourage exploration
- **Budget:** ~75 cycles/night × 8 min/cycle
- **Status:** Running autonomous experiments with crash-safe JSONL logging

#### 4. **LLM-Based Kernel Optimization Research**
- **File:** `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/optimization/llm_kernel_optimization_research.md`
- **Status:** Research methodology documented
- **Approaches Outlined:**
  - LLM4Sys (ASPLOS 2024): Using LLMs to optimize system performance
  - Neural Compiler (MLSys 2023): LLM-guided compiler optimizations
  - LLM-based AutoTVM (NeurIPS 2023): Learned cost models for tensor programs
  - Prompt-based kernel generation using CodeLlama/StarCoder
  - Learned cost models with GNNs and Transformer-based code-to-performance mapping

#### 5. **R-Zero Metrics Integration**
- **File:** `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/optimization/r_zero.py`
- **Status:** Minimal but present
- **Components:**
  - `RZeroMetrics`: Tracks success_rate, iteration_count, difficulty_adjustment
  - `LocalModelOptimizer`: Connects R-Zero metrics to local Ollama models (Qwen3-Coder, DeepSeek-R1)
  - Difficulty multiplier calculation based on trailing success rate

#### 6. **R-Zero Dashboard**
- **File:** `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/notebooks/marimo/r_zero_dashboard.py`
- **Status:** Interactive visualization in development
- **Features:**
  - Gateway unlock status tracking
  - Challenger difficulty gauge
  - Coherence trajectory visualization
  - Learning extraction status
  - Browser agent mining integration

---

### **What's MISSING**

#### 1. **Direct chengsong-huang/R-Zero Paper Implementation**
- **Gap:** No explicit references to the R-Zero paper at chengsong-huang.github.io/R-Zero
- **Missing:** Direct integration of R-Zero's reinforcement learning approach for GPU kernel generation
- **Note:** The codebase implements a *conceptually similar* co-evolutionary framework (Challenger/Solver) but doesn't explicitly cite or implement the specific RL methodology from the paper

#### 2. **End-to-End RL-Guided Kernel Generation Pipeline**
- **What exists:** K-Search tree for parameter exploration
- **What's missing:** RL agent that learns to *generate* new kernel code based on reward signals
- **Gap:** Current pipeline uses templates + parameter perturbation, not RL policy learning
- **Implication:** No policy gradient optimization (PPO, A3C, etc.) for kernel generation itself

#### 3. **Learned Performance Predictor (Reward Model for Kernel Performance)**
- **What exists:** Reward model for universe simulations (trajectory-based)
- **What's missing:** Learned cost model specifically for GPU kernel performance prediction
- **Gap:** The LLM optimization research outlines this, but it's not implemented
- **Current approach:** Empirical evaluation via popcorn-cli; no learned surrogate model

#### 4. **Multi-Step RL Episode Structure**
- **What exists:** Single-step kernel evaluation (template → submit → benchmark)
- **What's missing:** Multi-step RL episodes where an agent iteratively refines kernels based on cumulative reward
- **Gap:** Current K-Search doesn't track trajectory of refinements; each node is independent

#### 5. **Policy Network for Kernel Code Generation**
- **What exists:** Template-based generation with parameter tables
- **What's missing:** Neural network that outputs actual kernel code (not just parameters)
- **Gap:** Would require training a policy on kernel code embeddings + performance feedback

#### 6. **DPO (Direct Preference Optimization) Applied to Kernels**
- **What exists:** DPO infrastructure for universe trajectories
- **What's missing:** DPO training data specifically for kernel code preferences
- **Gap:** The PreferencePairGenerator works on trajectories, not kernel variants

---

### **Integration Gaps: Why K-Search Isn't R-Zero**

The K-Search system is **tree-search with heuristic priority**, not **reinforcement learning**:

| Aspect | K-Search (Current) | R-Zero / RL (Missing) |
|--------|-------------------|----------------------|
| Search | Priority-weighted tree nodes | RL policy learning (PPO/A3C) |
| Rewards | Used post-hoc for priority updates | Used in real-time policy gradient |
| Learning | Stagnation tracking + priority boost | Learned value/policy networks |
| Action Space | Template parameter perturbation | Generate novel kernel code |
| Episode Structure | Single evaluation → update tree | Multi-step refinement → cumulative reward |
| Exploration | Random perturbations + priority decay | Epsilon-greedy or entropy regularization |

---

### **Recommendations for R-Zero Integration**

If the goal is to implement the chengsong-huang R-Zero paper for GPU kernel optimization:

1. **Build a Learned Cost Model:**
   - Use GNN on kernel AST + performance counters
   - Train on K-Search results (3-5K kernel variants so far)
   - Use as reward signal for RL policy

2. **Implement Kernel Policy Network:**
   - Transformer-based generator: (kernel_context, target_perf) → kernel_code
   - RL training loop: generate → evaluate → backward through reward

3. **Multi-Step Episodes:**
   - State: current kernel + performance metrics
   - Action: propose optimization (vectorization, tiling, fusion, etc.)
   - Reward: (perf_improvement) - (code_length_penalty)
   - Episode: 3-5 refinement steps per kernel

4. **Connect to R-Zero Framework:**
   - Challenger: Propose harder kernels (larger M, longer sequences)
   - Solver: Use RL policy to generate optimized kernels
   - Judge: Compare against reference implementation + profile metrics

---

### **Files Relevant to R-Zero Vision**

**Core Infrastructure:**
- `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/optimization/r_zero.py` (metrics)
- `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/universe/llm_training_bridge.py` (reward signals)
- `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/notebooks/marimo/r_zero_dashboard.py` (visualization)

**Kernel Optimization Pipeline:**
- `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/autoresearch/driver.py`
- `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/autoresearch/ksearch_tree.py`
- `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/optimization/llm_kernel_optimization_research.md`

**Theory:**
- `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/docs/archive/RESEARCH_PAPER_DRAFT.md` (R-Zero Protocol)

---

**Bottom Line:** The codebase implements the *spirit* of R-Zero (co-evolution, difficulty scaling, reward signals) but applies it to universe simulations rather than GPU kernels. The kernel optimization pipeline uses deterministic tree-search + templates, not RL. **There's no learned policy network for kernel generation, no multi-step RL episodes, and no explicit implementation of the chengsong-huang paper methodology.** The foundation is there; the RL layer for kernel code synthesis is missing.

## Cohezion Autoresearch Architecture Report

Based on my thorough analysis of the codebase, here's the complete picture of your autonomous research system:

---

### **System Architecture Overview**

Your autoresearch implementation is located at:
- **Path**: `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/autoresearch/`
- **Purpose**: Autonomous overnight kernel optimization loop for AMD E2E Model Speedrun competition
- **Scope**: 3 kernels (MoE, GEMM, MLA) with ~75 experiment cycles per night

### **Core Components**

**1. K-Search Tree (`ksearch_tree.py`, 147 lines)**
- Tree-structured search with KNode dataclass (id, strategy, parameters, priority, status)
- Operations: `select_best()`, `update_result()`, `mark_failed()`, `decay_priorities()`
- **Stagnation-based pruning**: K=7 threshold — prunes nodes after 7 consecutive non-improving attempts
- Status tracking: `active`, `exhausted`, `pruned`
- Persistence: JSON serialization for crash recovery
- Priority mechanism: Boosted on improvement (stagnation_count reset), decayed periodically to encourage exploration

**2. Template-Based Generator (`generator.py`, 85 lines)**
- Uses Python's `string.Template` for `$PARAM` substitution (avoids escaping issues)
- Per-shape lookup tables instead of single global parameter
- Template variants:
  - **MoE**: `$KSPLIT_TABLE` (shape → KSPLIT 0-6), `$DEFAULT_KSPLIT`
  - **GEMM**: `$KERNEL_TABLE` (shape → {kernel, log2_ks}), `$DEFAULT_KERNEL`
  - **MLA**: `$SPLITS_TABLE` (shape → num_splits), `$KV_FORMAT`, `$FAST_MODE`
- Validates generated code syntax via `ast.parse()`
- Outputs to staging directory before overwriting `submission.py`

**3. Popcorn-CLI Evaluator (`evaluator.py`, 198 lines)**
- Wraps `~/.local/bin/popcorn-cli` with three modes:
  - **test**: Correctness check (no rate limit)
  - **benchmark**: Per-shape timings + geometric mean (no rate limit)
  - **leaderboard**: Official score (1/hour/problem rate limited)
- Output parsing: Extracts geomean from multiple formats (⏱ emoji, "geomean:" lines, tabular)
- Timeout: 12 minutes per evaluation
- Returns `EvalResult` with success/failure, per_shape_us dict, error messages

**4. Driver Loop (`driver.py`, 357 lines)**
- **Selection strategy**: Weighted random kernel rotation (MoE 50%, GEMM 30%, MLA 20%)
- **Per-cycle pipeline**:
  1. Select kernel via weighted rotation
  2. SELECT best node from K-Search tree
  3. Generate variant parameters (first attempt uses node params, subsequent attempts perturb)
  4. Generate submission.py from template
  5. Test via popcorn-cli
  6. If pass: Benchmark
  7. Analyze results → update tree priorities
  8. Log to JSONL (append-only, crash-safe)
  9. If new best AND rate limit allows: Submit to leaderboard
  10. Restore backup if not new best
- **Convergence detection**: If no >1% improvement in 10 cycles, mark kernel as converged
- **Cycle time**: ~8 minutes per cycle (70+ cycles overnight budget)
- **Priority decay**: Every 5 cycles, decay all active node priorities by ×0.95 to encourage exploration

**5. Result Analyzer (`analyzer.py`, 142 lines)**
- Computes `Analysis` dataclass: geomean_us, bottleneck_shape, improvement_pct, per_shape_rank, suggestion
- Identifies bottleneck shape (highest absolute time contributing to geomean)
- Generates next experiment suggestions based on bottleneck
- Logs to JSONL with full context (node_id, strategy, parameters, results, analysis)

**6. Rate Limiter (`rate_limiter.py`)**
- Enforces 1 leaderboard submission per hour per problem
- Tracks submission timestamps in memory (not persisted)
- Used to gate leaderboard submissions for "new best" results only

**7. Parameter Perturbation Strategy (`driver.py` lines 87-140)**
- After first attempt, subsequent attempts modify one random lookup table entry:
  - **MoE**: KSPLIT ±1-2 for random shape
  - **GEMM**: log2_ks ±1 or kernel swap for random shape
  - **MLA**: splits ×2, ÷2, ±4 for random shape
- Explores neighborhood of working configuration without explosive search space

---

### **Comparison to Karpathy's Autoresearch Pattern**

| Aspect | Karpathy Pattern | Your Implementation | Match? |
|--------|------------------|-------------------|--------|
| **Autonomous loop** | Hypothesis → experiment → analyze → iterate | ✓ Driver loop, K-Search tree for hypothesis selection | **YES** |
| **Hypothesis generation** | LLM-based or search-based | K-Search tree with priority-weighted selection | **YES** (tree-based) |
| **Experiment execution** | Local or remote evaluation | popcorn-cli remote MI355X evaluation | **YES** |
| **Result analysis** | Structured outcome recording | JSONL logging + bottleneck analysis | **YES** |
| **Iteration guidance** | Analyze → suggest next experiment | K-Search priority updates + stagnation pruning | **YES** |
| **Crash resilience** | Checkpoint/recovery | JSONL append-only logs, JSON tree persistence | **YES** |
| **Multi-kernel scheduling** | Single task focus | Weighted kernel rotation (MoE/GEMM/MLA) | **Extended** |
| **Parameter perturbation** | Guided local search | Random per-shape lookup table modification | **YES** |
| **Convergence detection** | Stop when plateau detected | >1% improvement threshold, K=7 stagnation pruning | **YES** |

**Key incorporated concepts**:
- ✓ Hypothesis generation (K-Search SELECT)
- ✓ Experiment automation (template generator + popcorn-cli)
- ✓ Result analysis (bottleneck detection, improvement tracking)
- ✓ Loop iteration (ANALYZE → UPDATE → SELECT)
- ✓ Parameter perturbation (local neighborhood exploration)
- ✓ Convergence tracking (stagnation threshold, >1% improvement gate)
- ✓ Multi-session coordination (staging directory, COORDINATION.md registry)

**Distinctive extensions beyond Karpathy's base pattern**:
1. **K-Search tree structure** — not pure random sweep; tree with priority-weighted selection and stagnation-based pruning
2. **Per-shape parameter tables** — generic KSPLIT value per shape, not global parameter
3. **Test-then-benchmark gating** — correctness check before expensive benchmarking
4. **Rate-limited leaderboard submission** — only submit on new best to avoid API abuse
5. **Backup/restore strategy** — staging directory protocol, rollback on non-improvement
6. **Multi-kernel scheduler** — weighted rotation across 3 kernels with convergence reallocation

---

### **Concepts NOT Incorporated**

- **LLM-based hypothesis generation**: Your K-Search is human-pre-seeded trees (11-14 nodes per kernel). No LLM synthesizes hypotheses.
- **Adversarial co-evolution** (R-Zero pattern): No competing agent feedback loop; single autoresearch agent
- **Online skill refinement**: No PRIME skill updates; experiments are hard-coded template variations
- **Journey tracking / 12D universe**: No integration with Cohezion's compound engineering loop
- **Vault-first learning**: No automatic skill extraction from experiment results; results stay in JSONL

---

### **Key Untried Levers (from Plan)**

Documented in `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/docs/plans/2026-03-19-autoresearch-ksearch-optimization.md`:

1. **GEMM A-quantization caching** (GAME-CHANGER) — `dynamic_mxfp4_quant(A)` costs 10-13µs per call; caching `A_q` on data_ptr() could save ~10µs → halve GEMM time
2. **MLA adaptive `num_kv_splits` table** — optimal varies 16x by shape; current hardcodes 16/8
3. **MoE KSPLIT validation** — KSPLIT env var may be dead code on runner; must validate before search
4. **GEMM per-shape kernel+split-K lookup** — 6 shapes × 2 kernels × 5 split-K values
5. **MLA MXFP4 vs FP8 KV comparison** — FP8 may be faster for small kv_seq_len

---

### **Integration with Cohezion**

Currently **isolated from** Cohezion's main compound engineering loop:
- Lives in `research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/`, not `src/cohezion/`
- No connection to SwarmExecutor, RequestAlignmentAnalyzer, or JourneyTracker
- No PRIME skill definitions (only templates)
- No vault learnings extraction

**Potential integration points**:
- Wrap autoresearch as a Cohezion skill (hypothesis generation + execution)
- Log experiments to JourneyTracker for journey coherence tracking
- Extract K-Search patterns to vault for future optimization tasks
- Use RequestAlignmentAnalyzer to decompose multi-kernel scheduling

---

### **File Paths**

Core implementation:
- `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/autoresearch/driver.py` (357 lines, main loop)
- `/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/autoresearch/ksearch_tree.py` (K-Search tree)

Templates:

# Karpathy's Autoresearch Architecture

## (1) Core Loop Structure
The system operates on a simple iterative cycle: AI agents modify code, run 5-minute training experiments, evaluate results, and decide whether to keep or discard changes. This repeats autonomously overnight, generating approximately 12 experiments per hour.

## (2) Hypothesis Generation
Agents generate hypotheses by modifying a single Python file (`train.py`) containing the model architecture, optimizer settings, and training hyperparameters. The instructions come from `program.md`, which serves as "baseline instructions for one agent" that humans can iterate on to guide research direction.

## (3) Experiment Evaluation
Experiments use a fixed metric: **val_bpb** (validation bits per byte), where lower values indicate better performance. This metric is "vocab-size-independent so architectural changes are fairly compared," enabling fair assessment regardless of what the agent modifies.

## (4) Iteration Process
The system employs a fixed 5-minute time budget per experiment. This constraint makes "experiments directly comparable regardless of what the agent changes" and ensures the approach "will find the most optimal model for your platform in that time budget."

## (5) LLM Integration
The framework expects Claude or similar coding models with file modification capabilities, invoked through prompts like: "Hi have a look at program.md and let's kick off a new experiment!"

## (6) Key Design Philosophy
The central insight: programming "research org code" (instructions and constraints) rather than touching Python directly. This separates human research strategy from automated execution, creating an "autonomous swarms of AI agents" conducting continuous optimization.
```

# K-Search Technical Methodology

## 1. Pi_plan (World Model): Strategy Reasoning

The world model, instantiated from an LLM, maintains an explicit search tree with two node types:

- **Closed nodes**: Completed actions with attached program implementations
- **Open nodes**: Pending optimization intents (e.g., "fuse_multi_head") awaiting execution

The model performs three tree operations:
- **INSERT**: Propose new child nodes extending promising branches
- **UPDATE**: Re-evaluate priority scores based on execution feedback
- **PRUNE**: Remove infeasible or redundant branches

As described: "the World Model is responsible for maintaining the search frontier and estimating the priority scores of high-level optimization intents" and is "co-evolving with the search process."

## 2. Pi_code (Code Instantiation)

Upon selecting an action, the system "employs the LLM as a stochastic policy π_code" to repeatedly sample concrete implementations: `x ~ π_code(· | a_t)` until a stagnation condition triggers. This isolates "logical reasoning from implementation details" by treating code generation as local refinement rather than a planning task.

## 3. V-Score (Priority Ranking)

The world model assigns scalar priority scores `V(a | S_t) ∈ [0,1]` to each frontier action. These scores represent "the model's intrinsic assessment of the potential of the actions" and are dynamically updated during evolution (e.g., scores can drop from 0.9→0.6 with new evidence).

## 4. Stagnation Detection

The parameter **K** is a "stagnation limit" (set to K=7 in main experiments). The system samples implementations until "K consecutive attempts without improvement" occur. This threshold prevents discarding valid strategies due to transient syntax errors while managing budget constraints.

## 5. Co-Evolution Loop

The iterative workflow:
1. **Selection**: Choose highest V-score action from frontier
2. **Instantiation**: Sample implementations via π_code until stagnation (K failed attempts)
3. **Evolution**: LLM analyzes execution trajectory, updating `S_{t+1}` via INSERT/UPDATE/PRUNE

"The world model continuously refines its understanding and beliefs based on past trials and accumulated experience via in-context learning," enabling dynamic strategy repositioning.

## 6. Action Selection Mechanism

Actions are selected deterministically at each iteration: `a_t = arg max_{a ∈ A(S_t)} V(a | S_t)`. This greedy approach prioritizes the frontier action with the highest estimated value, where V is contextually updated by the world model after each execution phase.

## 7. Key Results

- **14.3× improvement** on MoE kernels versus OpenEvolve (44.1 vs. 3.09 score)
- **2.10× average gain** across FlashInfer kernels over OpenEvolve (56.13 vs. 26.68)
- **2.21× improvement** over ShinkaEvolve (56.13 vs. 25.37)
- **State-of-the-art GPUMODE TriMul**: 1030 µs on H100, surpassing prior solutions

## 8. Differentiation from Evolutionary Baselines

Traditional evolutionary methods (OpenEvolve, ShinkaEvolve) "treat the LLM merely as a stochastic code generator" and "search directly in the space of program implementations," relying on heuristics like MAP-Elites.

K-Search decouples planning from implementation: "high-level optimization intent" guides the search tree, while π_code handles low-level code synthesis. This enables navigation of "complex, non-monotonic optimization paths" without premature branch elimination due to implementation noise. The world model's intrinsic domain knowledge actively drives search strategy rather than passively generating variants.

# R-Zero Methodology Analysis

## 1. Challenger-Solver Co-Evolution Framework

The system initializes two independent models with distinct roles. The "Challenger is rewarded for proposing tasks near the edge of the Solver capability, and the Solver is rewarded for solving increasingly challenging tasks posed by the Challenger." This adversarial dynamic creates a self-improving loop without external supervision.

## 2. Difficulty Generation and Calibration

The framework generates difficulty automatically through the interaction mechanism itself. Rather than using predefined curricula, the Challenger learns to propose problems calibrated to the Solver's current competency boundary—essentially discovering the edge of solvability through trial and feedback.

## 3. Reward/Training Signal

The training signals emerge from the co-evolutionary process: challenge quality (difficulty calibration) and solution success (task completion). The paper indicates this produces "a targeted, self-improving curriculum without any pre-existing tasks and labels."

## 4. Application Domains

Demonstrated improvements span:
- Math reasoning: +6.49 points on math benchmarks (Qwen3-4B-Base)
- General reasoning: +7.54 points across broader domains

The abstract doesn't detail code generation or other specialized domains.

## 5. Key Benchmark Results

Performance gains on Qwen3-4B-Base: +6.49 (math), +7.54 (general reasoning). The framework "substantially improves reasoning capability across different backbone LLMs."

## 6. GPU Kernel Optimization Relationship

**Not addressed.** The paper focuses on LLM training methodology and contains no discussion of GPU kernel optimization.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The three references serve very different roles in our pipeline:**

1. **K-Search (2602.19128)** — Directly applicable to GPU kernel optimization. Our autoresearch implements ~60% of it (tree structure, stagnation K=7, priority selection) but is missing the LLM intelligence layer (pi_plan world model that does INSERT/UPDATE/PRUNE, pi_code that generates novel code variants). This is the highest-impact gap.

2. **R-Zero (2508.05004)** — NOT about GPU kernels. It's a Challenger-Solver co-evolution framework for LLM training (math reasoning). Our codebase has a conceptual R-Zero implementation (`r_zero.py`, research paper draft) but for *universe simulations*, not kernels. The Challenger pattern could enhance K-Search's world model by proposing increasingly harder optimization targets.

3. **Karpathy's autoresearch** — Design philosophy match is strong (5-min experiments, val_bpb metric, program.md instructions, overnight autonomous loops). Our implementation follows this pattern well. The gap is that Karpathy's uses LLM agents to modify code directly, while ours uses deterministic templates.

**The common gap across all three: our autoresearch is a deterministic template engine, not an LLM-guided code synthesis system.**
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**The plan addresses the single biggest architectural gap:** our autoresearch is a deterministic template engine pretending to be K-Search. The real K-Search paper uses LLMs for both strategy planning (pi_plan) and code generation (pi_code). Adding Ollama integration (free, local, ~4s per call) transforms it from "parameter sweep" to "LLM-guided search with co-evolving world model" — the exact methodology that achieved 14.3x on MoE kernels in the paper.

The R-Zero integration is deliberately lightweight because the paper doesn't apply to GPU kernels — it's for LLM training. We take only the Challenger pattern (raise targets on plateau).

The Karpathy pattern is already 80% implemented. The missing 20% is `research_strategy.md` — a human-editable research direction file that guides the LLM agent.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`
